It was the 1960s, during the [Fifth French Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifth_French_Republic), that a considerable upturn in French neo-fascism occurred; some of it in response to the [Protests of 1968](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protests_of_1968). The most explicitly pro-Nazi of these was the [FANE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F%C3%A9d%C3%A9ration_d'action_nationale_et_europ%C3%A9enne) of [Mark Fredriksen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Fredriksen). Neo-fascist groups included [Pierre Sidos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre_Sidos)' [Occident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occident_\(movement\)), the [Ordre Nouveau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordre_Nouveau_\(1960s\)) (which was banned after violent clashes with the Trotskyist [LCR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolutionary_Communist_League_\(France\))) and the student-based [Groupe Union Défense](320279.md). A number of these activists such as [François Duprat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Duprat) were instrumental in founding the [Front National](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Front_\(France\)) under [Jean-Marie Le Pen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Marie_Le_Pen); but the FN also included a broader selection from the French hard-right, including not only these neo-fascist elements, but also [Catholic integrists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integrism), monarchists, Algerian War veterans, [Poujadists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poujadists) and national-conservatives. Others from these neo-fascist micro-groups formed the [Parti des forces nouvelles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parti_des_forces_nouvelles) working against Le Pen.

Within the FN itself, Duprat founded the FANE-backed [Groupes nationalistes révolutionnaires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolutionary_Nationalist_Groups) faction, until his 1978 assassination. The subsequent history of the French hard right has been the conflict between the national-conservative controlled FN and "national revolutionary" (fascistic and National Bolshevik) splinter or opposition groups. The latter include groups in the tradition of Thiriart and Duprat, such as the [Parti communautaire national-européen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parti_communautaire_national-europ%C3%A9en), [Troisième voie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troisi%C3%A8me_voie), the [Nouvelle Résistance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nouvelle_R%C3%A9sistance) of [Christian Bouchet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Bouchet),[^cite_note-Banquet-151] [Unité Radicale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unit%C3%A9_Radicale) and most recently [Bloc identitaire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloc_identitaire). Direct splits from the FN include the 1987 founded FANE-revival [Parti nationaliste français et européen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parti_nationaliste_fran%C3%A7ais_et_europ%C3%A9en), which was disbanded in 2000. Neo-Nazi organizations are outlawed in the Fifth French Republic, yet a significant number of them still exist.[^cite_note-152]

#### Germany

Further information: [Far-right politics in Germany (1945–present)](18526492.md)



Neo-Nazi demonstration in [Leipzig](17955.md), Germany, in October 2009

Following the failure of the [National Democratic Party of Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Democratic_Party_of_Germany) in the [election of 1969](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_German_federal_election,_1969), small groups committed to the revival of Nazi ideology began to emerge in Germany. The NPD splintered, giving rise to paramilitary *Wehrsportgruppe*. These groups attempted to organize under a national umbrella organization, the [Action Front of National Socialists/National Activists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Action_Front_of_National_Socialists/National_Activists).[^cite_note-virchow-153] Neo-Nazi movements in [East Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Germany) began as a rebellion against the Communist regime; the banning of Nazi symbols helped neo-Nazism to develop as an [anti-authoritarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-authoritarian) youth movement.[^cite_note-154] Mail order networks developed to send illegal Nazi-themed music [cassettes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compact_Cassette) and merchandise to Germany.[^cite_note-155]

[Turks in Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turks_in_Germany) have been victims of neo-Nazi violence on several occasions. In 1992, two young girls were killed in the [Mölln arson attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%B6lln_arson_attack?action=edit\&redlink=1) along with their grandmother; nine others were injured.[^cite_note-156][^cite_note-157] In 1993, five Turks were killed in the [Solingen arson attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1993_Solingen_arson_attack).[^cite_note-158] In response to the fire Turkish youth in Solingen rioted chanting "Nazis out!" and "We want Nazi blood". In other parts of Germany police had to intervene to protect [skinheads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skinhead) from assault.[^cite_note-159] The [Hoyerswerda riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoyerswerda_riots) and [Rostock-Lichtenhagen riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rostock-Lichtenhagen_riots) targeting migrants and ethnic minorities living in Germany also took place during the 1990s.[^cite_note-virchow-153]

Between 2000 and 2007, eight Turkish immigrants, one [Greek German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greeks_in_Germany) and a German policewoman were murdered by the neo-Nazi [National Socialist Underground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialist_Underground).[^cite_note-160] The NSU has its roots in the former East German area of [Thuringia](31130.md), which *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* identified as "one of the heartlands of Germany's radical right". The German intelligence services have been criticized for extravagant distributions of cash to informants within the far-right movement. Tino Brandt publicly boasted on television that he had received around €100,000 in funding from the German state. Though Brandt did not give the state "useful information", the funding supported recruitment efforts in Thuringia during the early 1990s. (Brandt was eventually sentenced to five and a half years in prison on for 66 counts of [child prostitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_prostitution) and [child sexual abuse](18721790.md)).[^cite_note-meaney-161]

Police were only able to locate the killers when they were tipped off following a botched bank robbery in [Eisenach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eisenach). As the police closed in on them, the two men committed suicide. They had evaded capture for 13 years. [Beate Zschäpe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beate_Zsch%C3%A4pe), who had been living with the two men in [Zwickau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zwickau), turned herself in to the German authorities a few days later. Zschäpe's trial began in May 2013; she was charged with nine counts of murder. She pleaded "not guilty". According to *The Guardian*, the NSU may have enjoyed protection and support from certain "elements of the state". [Anders Behring Breivik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anders_Behring_Breivik), a fan of Zschäpe's, reportedly sent her a letter from prison in 2012.[^cite_note-meaney-161]

According to the annual report of Germany's interior intelligence service (Verfassungsschutz) for 2012, at the time there were 26,000 right-wing extremists living in Germany, including 6,000 neo-Nazis.[^cite_note-162] In January 2020, Combat 18 was banned in Germany, and raids directed against the organization were made across the country.[^cite_note-163] In March 2020, United German Peoples and Tribes, which is part of [Reichsbürger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reichsb%C3%BCrger_movement), a neo-Nazi movement that rejects the German state as a legal entity, was raided by the German police.[^cite_note-164] [Holocaust denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_denial) is a crime, according to the German Criminal Code ([Strafgesetzbuch § 86a](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strafgesetzbuch_%C2%A7_86a)) and [§ 130 (public incitement)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laws_against_Holocaust_denial#Germany).

#### Greece



Flag of the [Golden Dawn](1938004.md)

The far-right political party [Golden Dawn](1938004.md) (Χρυσή Αυγή – Chrysi Avyi) is generally labelled neo-Nazi, although the group rejects this label.[^cite_note-165] A few Golden Dawn members participated in the [Bosnian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosnian_War) in the [Greek Volunteer Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Volunteer_Guard) (GVG) and were present in [Srebrenica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srebrenica) during the [Srebrenica massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srebrenica_massacre#Greek_Volunteers_controversy).[^cite_note-Michas-166][^cite_note-srebr-167] The party has its roots in Papadopoulos' regime.

There is often collaboration between the state and neo-Nazi elements in Greece.[^cite_note-dalakoglou-168] In 2018, during the trial of sixty-nine members of the Golden Dawn party, evidence was presented of the close ties between the party and the [Hellenic Police](10063533.md).[^cite_note-aj-169]

Golden Dawn has spoken out in favour of the [Assad regime](364813.md) in Syria,[^cite_note-170] and the Strasserist group Black Lily have claimed to have sent mercenaries to Syria to fight alongside the Syrian regime, specifically mentioning their participation in the [Battle of al-Qusayr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_al-Qusayr_\(2013\)).[^cite_note-171] In the [6 May 2012 legislative election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_legislative_election,_May_2012), Golden Dawn received 6.97% of the votes, entering the Greek parliament for the first time with 21 representatives, but when the elected parties were unable to form a [coalition government](6036.md) a [second election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_legislative_election,_June_2012) was held in June 2012. Golden Dawn received 6.92% of the votes in the June election and entered the [Greek parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_parliament) with 18 representatives.

Since 2008, neo-Nazi violence in Greece has targeted [immigrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_to_Greece), [leftists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leftist) and [anarchist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anarchism_in_Greece) activists. In 2009, certain far-right groups announced that [Agios Panteleimonas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agios_Panteleimonas,_Athens) in Athens was off limits to immigrants. Neo-Nazi patrols affiliated with the Golden Dawn party began attacking migrants in this neighborhood. The violence continued escalating through 2010.[^cite_note-dalakoglou-168] In 2013, after the murder of [anti-fascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-fascist) rapper [Pavlos Fyssas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pavlos_Fyssas), the number of [hate crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hate_crimes) in Greece declined for several years until 2017. Many of the crimes in 2017 have been attributed to other groups like the Crypteia Organisation and Combat 18 Hellas.[^cite_note-aj-169]

Golden Dawn was banned in 2020 for multiple violent crimes and murders. However, multiple successor parties emerged and secured representation in the Hellenic parliament. Given that many of [Spartans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spartans_\(Greek_political_party\))' members of parliament have previously been associated with either [Golden Dawn](1938004.md) or Kasidiaris' party [Greeks for the Fatherland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greeks_for_the_Fatherland), which was banned from participating in the 2023 elections, Spartans has been seen as a continuation of Golden Dawn.[^cite_note-172]

On June 28, 2025 [Thanos Plevris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thanos_Plevris) was appointed the new migration minister. Plevris has previously burned Turkish flags together with the neo-Nazi Golden Dawn, advocated shooting migrants and promoted "purity" of the "Greek race".[^cite_note-WSWS-173][^cite_note-174][^cite_note-175][^cite_note-auto-176] The Central Board of Jewish Communities had earlier voiced concerns about Plevris since he had defended the statement that Auschwitz should be re-opened for Jewish inmates, arguing that advocating for the extermination of the Jewish people should be legal.[^cite_note-WSWS-173]

#### Hungary



"Hungaria Skins" with a flag evoking the Arrow Cross in 1997

In Hungary, the historical political party which allied itself ideologically with German National Socialism and drew inspiration from it, was the [Arrow Cross Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrow_Cross_Party) of [Ferenc Szálasi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferenc_Sz%C3%A1lasi). They referred to themselves explicitly as National Socialists and within Hungarian politics this tendency is known as [Hungarism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarism). After the Second World War, exiles such as [Árpád Henney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%81rp%C3%A1d_Henney) kept the Hungarist tradition alive. Following the fall of the [Hungarian People's Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian_People's_Republic) in 1989, which was a [Marxist–Leninist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxist%E2%80%93Leninist) state and a member of the [Warsaw Pact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw_Pact), many new parties emerged. Amongst these was the [Hungarian National Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian_National_Front) of [István Győrkös](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istv%C3%A1n_Gy%C5%91rk%C3%B6s), which was a Hungarist party and considered itself the heirs of Arrow Cross-style National Socialism (a self-description they explicitly embraced). In the 2000s, Győrkös' movement moved closer to a [national bolshevist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_bolshevism) and [neo-Eurasian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-Eurasian) position, aligned with [Aleksandr Dugin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksandr_Dugin), cooperating with the [Hungarian Workers' Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian_Workers'_Party). Some Hungarists opposed this and founded the [Pax Hungarica Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pax_Hungarica_Movement).

In 2008-2009 four Hungarian [neo-Nazis killed six Romanis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008%E2%80%932009_neo-Nazi_murders_of_Roma_in_Hungary) and seriously injured five with guns, grenades and Molotov cocktails.[^cite_note-177]

In modern Hungary, [Jobbik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jobbik) was regarded by some scholars as a neo-Nazi party; for example, it had been termed as such by [Randolph L. Braham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Randolph_L._Braham).[^cite_note-178] The party denied being neo-Nazi, although "there is extensive proof that the leading members of the party made no effort to hide their racism and anti-Semitism."[^cite_note-Spectrum-179] Rudolf Paksa, a scholar of the Hungarian far-right, described Jobbik as "anti-Semitic, racist, homophobic and chauvinistic" but not as neo-Nazi because it does not pursue the establishment of a totalitarian regime.[^cite_note-Spectrum-179] Historian [Krisztián Ungváry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kriszti%C3%A1n_Ungv%C3%A1ry) writes that "It is safe to say that certain messages of Jobbik can be called open neo-Nazi propaganda. However, it is quite certain that the popularity of the party is not due to these statements."[^cite_note-180]

However, since 2014 Jobbik has moderated into center-right pro-European conservative party according to multiple sources.[^cite_note-181] The radical right-wing members of Jobbik disappointed with the more moderate direction defected and formed the [Our Homeland Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Homeland_Movement) (MHM).[^cite_note-182] MHM has been described as neo-fascist and they have celebrated the Arrow Cross nazis of the Second World War.[^cite_note-ERRC-183][^cite_note-neo-fascist-184] In the [2024 European Parliament election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_European_Parliament_election) MHM successfully secured representation, while the moderate Jobbik party failed to gain a seat.[^cite_note-ERRC-183]

#### Ireland

SS-Obersturmbannführer [Otto Skorzeny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Otto_Skorzeny) owned a 200-acre farm in Ireland that was suspected was used for paramilitary neo-Nazi practices in 1960.[^cite_note-185]

In 1966, the National Socialist Union of Ireland and the Irish National Socialist Movement merged and formed the National Socialist Federation. The NSF was led by A. L. Price, its party organ was *National Socialist News* and its youth wing the National Socialist Federation of Eire, led by Cyril Kavanagh.[^cite_note-186]

The [National Socialist Irish Workers Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialist_Irish_Workers_Party) (NSIWP), a small party, was active between 1968 and the late 1980s, producing neo-Nazi propaganda pamphlets and sending threatening messages to Jews and Black people living in Ireland.[^cite_note-187] The NSIWP sought to recruit [UNIFIL](787766.md) veterans "who had witnessed at first hand the devastation caused by Israeli power".[^cite_note-188]

A Maoist book shop in [Limerick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limerick) opened in 1970 and had its windows smashed out several times before gunshots were fired into it in March. There was also an attempted firebombing.[^cite_note-Hanley-189] "The National Movement", a neo-Nazi group began parading and campaigning for mayor [Stephen Coughlan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Coughlan), who had incited anti-communist hysteria in the city. The National Movement also gathered signatures for a petition aimed at shutting down the bookshop[^cite_note-Hanley-189] and distributed material by the [American Nazi Party](364176.md) while campaigning. Coughlan arranged the National Movement a hearing with Limerick Corporation, after which two Labour councillors called for a vote on a motion to thank them.[^cite_note-190][^cite_note-191] The National Movement openly sold their newspaper *Nationalist Worker* that praised Hitler on the streets.[^cite_note-192]

[Kilkenny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kilkenny)-based Irish National Socialist Party attempted to get in the party register in 1990. Leading member of the party Michael McGrath was also a [Celtic neopagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celtic_neopaganism) and an "Arch-druid". The party spread Nazi propaganda as a newspaper and leaflets.[^cite_note-193]

[Derek Turner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derek_Turner_\(journalist\)), the one time leader of the Irish neo-Nazi group, Social Action Initiative, wrote a glossy magazine *[Right Now!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_Now!_\(magazine\))* that catered to the right wing of the [Conservative party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Party_\(UK\)) and attacked the European Union and multiculturalism. Turner attracted contributions from a number of prominent Conservative MPs.[^cite_note-194][^cite_note-195] Turner has referred to himself as the "neighbourhood Nazi".[^cite_note-196]

American Ku Klux Klan Grand Dragon and a founding member of the neo-Nazi terrorist group [the Order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Order_\(white_supremacist_group\)) Frank Silva has staged speeches for and "mentors" Irish far-right activists.[^cite_note-197]

According to the [Tribune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribune_\(magazine\)), as of 2025, many openly far-right and neo-Nazi groups like Clann Eireann have appeared, and they now outnumber antifascist counterprotests during demonstrations. For example, Clann Eireann leader [Justin Barrett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justin_Barrett) praised Hitler as "the greatest leader of all time". In 2024 an immigrant was beaten to death in Dublin for not speaking English.[^cite_note-198][^cite_note-199]

#### Italy



The Italian group [Ordine Nuovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordine_Nuovo), banned in 1974, drew influence from the [Waffen-SS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waffen-SS) and [Guénonian Traditionalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditionalist_School) via [Julius Evola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julius_Evola)

During the 1950s, the neo-fascist [Italian Social Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Social_Movement) moved closer to bourgeois conservative politics on the domestic front, which led to radical youths founding hardline splinter groups, such as [Pino Rauti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pino_Rauti)'s [Ordine Nuovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordine_Nuovo) (later succeeded by [Ordine Nero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordine_Nero)) and [Stefano Delle Chiaie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefano_Delle_Chiaie)'s [Avanguardia Nazionale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Vanguard_\(Italy\)). These organisations were influenced by the esotericism of [Julius Evola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julius_Evola) and considered the Waffen-SS and Romanian leader [Corneliu Zelea Codreanu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corneliu_Zelea_Codreanu) a reference, moving beyond Italian fascism. They were implicated in [paramiliary attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Years_of_Lead_\(Italy\)) during the late 1960s to the early 1980s, such as the [Piazza Fontana bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piazza_Fontana_bombing). Delle Chiaie had even assisted [Junio Valerio Borghese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junio_Valerio_Borghese) in a failed 1970 coup attempt known as the [Golpe Borghese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golpe_Borghese), which attempted to reinstate a fascist state in Italy.

On December 4, 2024, in [Bologna](21069333.md) 25 houses belonging to the members of the neo-Nazi "[Nuovo Ordine Sociale-Sole Nero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomwaffen_Division#Italy_\(Nuovo_Ordine_Sociale\))" (New Social Order-Black Sun) or "Werwolf Division" were raided and members were arrested for plotting to murder [Giorgia Meloni](29228976.md) and possessing illegal firearms. According to [Il Giornale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Il_Giornale) the group was connected to jihadi terrorists. Allegedly a man from Bologna volunteered to fight in the Palestinian group [Lions' Den](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lions'_Den_\(militant_group\)) and returned to Italy, and a Palestinian PLO member Zyad Abu Saleh built explosives for the group.[^cite_note-200][^cite_note-201][^cite_note-202]

In early 2026, six members of the neo-Nazi group *Ordine Ario Romano* were formally indicted in [Rome](25458.md) on charges of propaganda *and* incitement to racial, ethnic and religious hatred, following a lengthy investigation that began with the group’s dismantling in 2021. Among those charged was Francesca Rizzi, known in media as “Miss Hitler” after winning an online competition on a Russian social network, along with Luigi Petricca, Remo Governatori, Gregory Rossi, Piersimone Volpe and Mario Marras, who were accused of disseminating racist and antisemitic content online and via messaging platforms. Prosecutors alleged that posts circulated by the group denied the [Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust), branded it “the greatest [lie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_denial) in history”, and contained calls for the elimination of Jews worldwide. The trial was scheduled to begin at the Tribunal of Rome on 4 June 2026, with civil parties including the [Union of Italian Jewish Communities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Italian_Jewish_Communities), ANPI and Senator for life Liliana Segre.[^cite_note-203][^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205]

#### Latvia

[Mārupe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C4%81rupe) municipal councillor and founder of the [Rising Sun for Latvia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rising_Sun_for_Latvia) party [Raivis Zeltīts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raivis_Zelt%C4%ABts) was a member of the neo-Nazi [Iron March](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_March) and appeared alongside [National Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Action_\(UK\)) members during [Latvian Legion Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latvian_Legion_Day) commemorating the [Latvian SS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latvian_Legion).[^cite_note-206][^cite_note-207][^cite_note-208]

The [World Jewish Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Jewish_Congress) has called for "decisive action" against "the mass neo-Nazi demonstrations" commemorating Latvian Nazi collaborators that take place annually in the capital [Riga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riga).[^cite_note-worldjewishcongress-209]

Latvian member of the Baltic terrorist organization [Feuerkrieg Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feuerkrieg_Division) Arturs Aispurs was charged with plotting bombing muslims during New Year celebrations.[^cite_note-FKD-210]

#### Lithuania

[Mindaugas Murza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mindaugas_Murza) was the leader of multiple self-identified "National Socialist" groups such as the "Lithuanian National Socialist Party" (LNSP). Murza and three other from LNSP were elected to the council of the [Šiauliai City Municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0iauliai_City_Municipality) in the 2003 municipal elections. Another member of LNSP was elected to the council of the [Alytus City Municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alytus_City_Municipality).[^cite_note-bns2003-211][^cite_note-212][^cite_note-213]

According to the [World Jewish Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Jewish_Congress) "more than 1,000 neo-Nazis paraded through the Lithuanian capital Vilnius chanting ‘Lithuanians for Lithuania’" in 2019.[^cite_note-worldjewishcongress-209]

Lithuanian member of the Baltic terrorist organization [Feuerkrieg Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feuerkrieg_Division) was charged with bombing financial institutions.[^cite_note-FKD-210] Lithuanian police also confiscated illegal firearms, Nazi paraphernalia and chemicals to make explosives from the man.[^cite_note-214]

#### Netherlands

Noteworthy neo-Nazi movements and parties in the Netherlands include the [National European Social Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_European_Social_Movement) (NESB), the [Dutch People's Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_People's_Union) (NVU),[^cite_note-215] the [National Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Alliance_\(Netherlands\)) (NA),[^cite_note-cp-216] and the [Nationalist People's Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalist_People's_Movement) (NVB). Individuals of note have included *Waffen-SS* volunteer and NESB founder [Paul van Tienen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_van_Tienen), war-time collaborator and NESB co-founder [Jan Wolthuis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Wolthuis), former NVU member [Bernhard Postma](https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernhard%20Postma), the "Black Widow" [Florentine Rost van Tonningen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florentine_Rost_van_Tonningen), former NVU leader [Joop Glimmerveen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joop_Glimmerveen),[^cite_note-nvu1-217] CP/CP'86 member and NVB leader [Wim Beaux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wim_Beaux), former CP/CP'86 member and NA leader [Jan Teijn](https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan%20Teijn), former NVU member and "Hitler-lookalike"[^cite_note-218] [Stefan Wijkamp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefan_Wijkamp?action=edit\&redlink=1), former CP'86 member and current NVU leader [Constant Kusters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constant_Kusters),[^cite_note-nvu1-217] and former NVU member and NA leader [Virginia Kapić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_Kapi%C4%87).

Both the [General Intelligence and Security Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Intelligence_and_Security_Service)[^cite_note-219] and non-governmental initiatives such as the far-left [anti-fascist research group Kafka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-fascist_research_group_Kafka) research neo-Nazism and other forms of political extremism and have attested to the local presence of international movements such as Blood & Honour,[^cite_note-220][^cite_note-221] Combat 18,[^cite_note-222] the [Racial Volunteer Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_Volunteer_Force),[^cite_note-223] and [The Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Base_\(hate_group\)),[^cite_note-224] and expressed concern at the online dissemination of alt-right and [far-right accelerationist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accelerationism#Far-right_accelerationist_terrorism) thought in the Netherlands.[^cite_note-225]

#### Poland



ONR employs imagery associated with Nazism: Uniformed skinheads, armbands and Celtic crosses

Under the [Polish Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Constitution) promoting any totalitarian system such as [Nazism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazism), [fascism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascism), or [communism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communism), as well as inciting violence and/or racial hatred is illegal.[^cite_note-226] This was further re-enforced in the [Polish Penal Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Penal_Code) where discrediting any group or persons on national, religious, or racial grounds carries a sentence of 3 years.[^cite_note-227]

Several far-right and anti-semitic organisations exist, most notably [NOP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Revival_of_Poland) and [ONR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Radical_Camp_\(1993\)) (both of which exist legally) and while they are classified as fascist, they officially say they are adherents of "[National Democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Democracy_\(Poland\))" rather than Nazism. These groups attempt to frame their activities as "patriotic" rather than neo-Nazi, even while employing Nazi symbolism or rhetoric, such as the [Roman salute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_salute), which they distinguish from the [Nazi salute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_salute).[^cite_note-dziennik.pl(2008-06-21)-228] However, Daniel Pładek, a sociologist at the Jagiellonian University and a researcher of the extreme right and [Anti-Defamation League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Defamation_League) describe NOP and ONR as "Nazi-like" or outright neo-Nazi, despite their claims to the contrary.[^cite_note-229][^cite_note-Dryjanska2-230] NOP was described as "overtly nazi" by anti-hate advocacy group [Hope not Hate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hope_not_Hate) and NOP is connected to the banned neo-Nazi terrorist group [National Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Action_\(UK\)).[^cite_note-231]



Polish neo-Nazis demonstrating, 2024

According to the ADL [Self-Defence of the Republic of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-Defence_of_the_Republic_of_Poland)-party that had at most 10% of the vote tolerates neo-Nazis among its ranks and its founder [Andrzej Lepper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrzej_Lepper) has praised Adolf Hitler. For example, Self-Defense MP Mateusz Piskorski has translated the texts of the [Order of Nine Angles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_Nine_Angles) leader [David Myatt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Myatt) into Polish.[^cite_note-232]

In addition to the fascist groups that tactically officially reject Nazism, there are several openly self-identified Nazi groups in Poland. For example, the Pride and Modernity-group organizes big events to celebrate the birthday of Adolf Hitler where they burn wooden swastikas.[^cite_note-233] The neo-Nazi gang Bad Company threw a welcoming party for [Janusz Waluś](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janusz_Walu%C5%9B), a right-wing extremist who assassinated the anti-apartheid black activist [Chris Hani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Hani).[^cite_note-234] Polish neo-Nazis from Association of Independence Rota held an event at the German border, opposing refugees coming from the West.[^cite_note-235] [Szturmowcy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Szturmowcy) (Stormtroopers) Nazi group held demonstrations, holding banners calling for a "White Europe".[^cite_note-236] Polish Nazis from Zadrużny Krąg have also fought as part of the pagan neo-nazi [Rusich Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rusich_Group).[^cite_note-237] In addition to these examples, many other neo-Nazi groups exist, like the National Socialist Front, the Front for National Cleansing and the National-Socialist Congress.[^cite_note-238][^cite_note-Cas-239]

Poland is also home to neo-Nazi bands such as [Graveland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graveland) and [Honor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honor_\(band\)).[^cite_note-240][^cite_note-241] Reportedly an album by a neo-Nazi band named Legion sold over 30,000 copies even before the fall of the Iron Curtain.[^cite_note-242]

[Robert Winnicki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Winnicki)'s [National Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Movement_\(Poland\)) sponsored the November 2017 anti-Israel demonstration that was attended by 60,000 people. [Algemeiner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algemeiner) characterized the demonstration as "Ultranationalist and neo-Nazi".[^cite_note-243] The [All-Polish Youth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All-Polish_Youth) is the unofficial youth group of the National Movement. The All-Polish Youth has also been linked to neo-Nazis and caused controversy when its members were saluting swastika flags and chanting "sieg heil".[^cite_note-244][^cite_note-245]

According to several reporter investigations, the Polish government turns a blind eye to these groups, and they are free to spread their ideology, frequently dismissing their existence as [conspiracy theories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conspiracy_theories), dismissing acts political provocations, deeming them too insignificant to pose a threat, or attempting to justify or diminish the seriousness of their actions.[^cite_note-246][^cite_note-247][^cite_note-248][^cite_note-249]

Former Polish ruling party [Law and Justice](520309.md) (PiS) allegedly facilitated co-operation between conservative institutions and far-right extremists. In 2023, the PiS affiliated fundamentalist Catholic group [Ordo Iuris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ordo_Iuris) started a campaign for the release of a neo-Nazi activist Marika Matuszak convicted of attacking an LGBT event, and she was released by PiS Justice Minister [Zbigniew Ziobro](5816176.md).[^cite_note-250][^cite_note-251] [Sejm](28167.md) member and chair of the [Together Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Together_Party) [Adrian Zandberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adrian_Zandberg) criticized PiS Prime Minister [Mateusz Morawiecki](48505487.md) for "commemorat\[ing] a unit that openly collaborated with the Gestapo" for paying tribute to the [Holy Cross Mountains Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Cross_Mountains_Brigade) and said [Hubert Jura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubert_Jura) may be a hero to Morawiecki, but not to him.[^cite_note-252]

#### Russia

Main article: [Neo-Nazism in Russia](45405712.md)

Further information: [Racism in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racism_in_Russia), [Radical nationalism in Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radical_nationalism_in_Russia), [Rashism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashism), and [Antisemitism in the Soviet Union § Right-wing movements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_the_Soviet_Union#Right-wing_movements)



A neo-Nazi in Russia at an anti-gay demonstration in Moscow, October 2010

Some observers have noted a subjective irony of Russians embracing Nazism, because one of Hitler's ambitions at the start of [World War II](32927.md) was the [Generalplan Ost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Generalplan_Ost) (Master Plan East) which envisaged to exterminate, expel, or enslave most or all Slavs from central and eastern Europe (e.g., Russians, Ukrainians, Poles etc.).[^cite_note-William1-253] At the end of the [Nazi invasion of the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_invasion_of_the_Soviet_Union), over 25 million Soviet citizens had died.[^cite_note-254]

The first reports of [neo-Nazi](45405712.md) organizations in the USSR appeared in the second half of the 1950s. In some cases, the participants were attracted primarily by the aesthetics of Nazism (rituals, parades, uniforms, the cult of physical fitness, architecture). Other organizations were more interested in the ideology of the Nazis, their program, and the image of [Adolf Hitler](2731583.md).[^cite_note-Charn-255] The formation of neo-Nazism in the USSR dates back to the turn of the 1960s and 1970s; during this period, these organizations still preferred to operate underground.

Modern [Russian neo-paganism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavic_Native_Faith) took shape in the second half of the 1970s[^cite_note-256] and is associated with the activities of supporters of antisemitism, especially the Moscow Arabist [Valery Yemelyanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valery_Yemelyanov) (also known as "Velemir") and the former dissident and neo-Nazi activist [Alexey Dobrovolsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexey_Dobrovolsky) (also known as "Dobroslav").

In Soviet times, the founder of the movement of [Peterburgian Vedism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peterburgian_Vedism) (a branch of Slavic neopaganism) Viktor Bezverkhy (Ostromysl) revered Hitler and [Heinrich Himmler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heinrich_Himmler) and propagated [racial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_racism) and [antisemitic theories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_antisemitism) in a narrow circle of his students, calling for the deliverance of mankind from "inferior offspring", allegedly arising from [interracial marriages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interracial_marriage). He called such "inferior people" "bastards", referred to them as "[Zhyds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhyd), Indians or gypsies and [mulattoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mulatto)" and believed that they prevent society from achieving social justice.

The first public manifestations of neo-Nazis in Russia took place in 1981 in [Kurgan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurgan), and then in [Yuzhnouralsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuzhnouralsk), [Nizhny Tagil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nizhny_Tagil), [Sverdlovsk](66524.md), and Leningrad.[^cite_note-obezyana-257][^cite_note-258]

In 1982, on Hitler's birthday, a group of Moscow high school students held a Nazi demonstration on [Pushkinskaya Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pushkinskaya_Square).[^cite_note-obezyana-257]

[Russian National Unity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_National_Unity) (RNE) was a neo-Nazi group founded in 1990 and was led by [Alexander Barkashov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Barkashov), who claimed to have members in 250 cities. RNE adopted the swastika as its symbol, and sees itself as the avant-garde of a coming national revolution. It is critical of other major far-right organizations, such as the [Liberal Democratic Party of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_Democratic_Party_of_Russia) (LDPR). As of 1997, the members RNE were called Soratnik (comrades in arms), receive combat training at locations near Moscow, and many of them work as security officers or armed guards.[^cite_note-259] RNE was banned in 1999 by Moscow's court in 1999,[^cite_note-Saunders-260] after which the group faded away.[^cite_note-261][^cite_note-262]

In 2007, it was claimed that Russian neo-Nazis accounted for "half of the world's total".[^cite_note-263][^cite_note-264]

On 15 August 2007, Russian authorities arrested a student for allegedly posting a video on the Internet which appears to show [two migrant workers being beheaded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murder_of_Shamil_Odamanov) in front of a red and black swastika flag.[^cite_note-265] Alexander Verkhovsky, the head of a Moscow-based center that monitors [hate crime](13547.md) in Russia, said, "It looks like this is the real thing. The killing is genuine ... There are similar videos from the Chechen war. But this is the first time the killing appears to have been done intentionally."[^cite_note-execution-266] The [Ministry of Internal Affairs](53056.md) has a history of downplaying and denying neo-Nazi violence: A ministry representative called the beheading video a "fake".[^cite_note-267][^cite_note-268] On November 4, 2025, a racially motivated murder took place in Moscow on the [Russian Unity Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Unity_Day). The "National Socialist Organization for the Liberation of White Europe" took credit and posted a video of the murder. The Ministry again downplayed it and stated it was a conflict between teens.[^cite_note-269]

[Atomwaffen Division Russland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomwaffen_Division_Russland) is a neo-Nazi terrorist group in Russia found by Russian officials to have been tied to multiple mass murder plots. AWDR was founded by former members of defunct [National Socialist Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialist_Society) responsible for 27 murders and AWDR is connected to local chapter of the Order of Nine Angles responsible for rapes, ritual murders and drug trafficking. The Russian authorities raided an Atomwaffen compound in [Ulan-Ude](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulan-Ude) and uncovered illegal weapons and explosives.[^cite_note-270][^cite_note-Fontaka-271][^cite_note-Fontaka2-272][^cite_note-273]

Neo-Nazi groups such as "88th Brigade" [Espanola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Espanola_\(battalion\)) and [Rusich Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rusich_Group) are taking part in the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-274][^cite_note-275] Rusich Group is connected to the Order of Nine Angles and they have been responsible for multiple crimes, including ritual murder.[^cite_note-276][^cite_note-odessa-277]

According to [The Daily Telegraph](23797577.md), Russian state militarization of the youth and narratives of external threats have been responsible for youth violence and school attacks against perceived outsiders. There were at least seven school attacks in the first two months of 2026, like the [Bashkir State Medical University attack](82360474.md) in which a teen neo-Nazi stabbed seven foreign students while shouting Nazi slogans and painting a swastika with the victims' blood. There is also a convergence with militants fighting in Ukraine, the neo-Nazi paramilitary Rusich group supported the 15-year-old white supremacist who stabbed a Tajik student to death in the [2025 Odintsovo school attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Odintsovo_school_attack).[^cite_note-278]

#### Serbia

An example of neo-Nazism in Serbia is the group [Nacionalni stroj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nacionalni_stroj). In 2006 charges were brought against 18 leading members.[^cite_note-279][^cite_note-280][^cite_note-281] Besides political parties, there are a few militant neo-Nazi organizations in Serbia, such as [Blood & Honour Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood_&_Honour) and [Combat 18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combat_18).[^cite_note-282] In 2019 Serbian Combat 18 was discovered to be trafficking firearms to [Atomwaffen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomwaffen) neo-Nazis selling handguns, assault rifles, grenades and RPG-7s from the Balkans to French neo-Nazis in [Marseille](40888948.md).[^cite_note-283][^cite_note-284][^cite_note-285]

[Serbian Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_Action) is a prominent neo-Nazi group in Serbia. The group adheres to the ideology of the fascist [ZBOR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ZBOR) and the [Serbian Nazi collaborators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collaboration_with_Nazi_Germany_and_Fascist_Italy#Territory_of_the_Military_Commander_in_Serbia) [Milan Nedic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milan_Nedic) and [Dimitrije Ljotić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dimitrije_Ljoti%C4%87) and Bishop [Nikolaj Velimirović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikolaj_Velimirovi%C4%87), an early supporter of Adolf Hitler. They also organize annual memorial events and marches for them.[^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287] Serbian Action is also proponent of the ideology of [accelerationism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accelerationism) and supports overthrowing the government in favor of Orthodox monarchy. Serbian Action is also affiliated with the neo-Nazi monarchist Russian Imperial Movement, [Golden Dawn](1938004.md) and the [Iron March](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_March) network that has been described as "terroristic".[^cite_note-288][^cite_note-terrorism-289]

#### Slovakia

Main article: [Kotlebists – People's Party Our Slovakia § Ties to fascism and far-right extremism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kotlebists_%E2%80%93_People's_Party_Our_Slovakia#Ties_to_fascism_and_far-right_extremism)

The Slovak political party [Kotlebists – People's Party Our Slovakia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kotlebists_%E2%80%93_People's_Party_Our_Slovakia), which is represented in the [National Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Council_\(Slovakia\)) and [European Parliament](9581.md), is widely characterized as neo-Nazi.[^cite_note-290][^cite_note-291][^cite_note-292] Kotleba has softened its image over time and now disputes that is fascist or neo-Nazi, even suing a media outlet that described it as neo-Nazi. As of 2020, the party spokesperson was Ondrej Durica, a former member of the neo-Nazi band *Biely Odpor* (White Resistance). 2020 candidate Andrej Medvecky was convicted of attacking a black man while shouting racial slurs; another candidate, Anton Grňo, was fined for making a [fascist salute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Na_str%C3%A1%C5%BE). The party still celebrates 14 March, the anniversary of the founding of the fascist first [Slovak Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slovak_Republic_\(1939%E2%80%931945\)).[^cite_note-fp-293] In 2020, party leader [Marian Kotleba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marian_Kotleba) was facing trial for writing checks for 1,488 euros, alleged to be a reference to [Fourteen Words](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourteen_Words) and [Heil Hitler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heil_Hitler).[^cite_note-294]

In the [2022 Bratislava shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Bratislava_shooting) a Slovak neo-Nazi shot and killed two patrons of a gay bar and injured others.[^cite_note-295]

#### Spain



Neo-Nazi skinheads in Spain

Spanish neo-Nazism is often connected to the country's [Francoist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francoist) and [Falangist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falangism) past, and nurtured by the ideology of the [National Catholicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Catholicism).[^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297]

According to a study by the [newspaper *ABC*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABC_\(Spain\)), [black people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Spanish_people) are the ones who have suffered the most attacks by neo-Nazi groups, followed by [Maghrebis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maghrebis) and [Latin Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_Americans). They have also caused deaths in the anti-fascist group, such as the murder of the Madrid-born sixteen-year-old [Carlos Palomino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murder_of_Carlos_Palomino) on 11 November 2007, stabbed with a knife by a soldier in the [Legazpi metro station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legazpi_\(Madrid_Metro\)) ([Madrid](41188263.md)).[^cite_note-298]

There have been other neo-Nazi cultural organizations such as the [Spanish Circle of Friends of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Circle_of_Friends_of_Europe) (CEDADE) and the Circle of Indo-European Studies (CEI).[^cite_note-299]

The extreme right has little electoral support, with the presence of these groups of 0.36% (if the [Plataforma per Catalunya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plataforma_per_Catalunya) (PxC) party is excluded with 66007 votes (0.39%), according to the voting data of the European elections of 2014. The first extreme right party [FE de las JONS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FE_de_las_JONS) obtains 0.13% of the votes (21 577 votes), after doubling its results after the crisis; this is followed by the far-right party La España en Marcha (LEM) with 0.1% of the votes, [National Democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Democracy_\(Spain\)) (DN) of the far-right with 0.08%, [Republican Social Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_Social_Movement) (MSR) (far-right) with 0.05% of the votes.[^cite_note-300]

#### Sweden

Neo-Nazi activities in Sweden have previously been limited to [white supremacist](33178.md) groups, few of which have a membership over a few hundred members.[^cite_note-301] The main neo-Nazi organization is the [Nordic Resistance Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_Resistance_Movement), a political movement which engages in martial arts training and paramilitary exercises[^cite_note-302] and which has been called a terrorist group.[^cite_note-303] They are also active in [Norway](21241.md) and [Denmark](76972.md); the branch in [Finland](10577.md) was banned in 2019.

#### Switzerland

See also: [Far-right politics in Switzerland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far-right_politics_in_Switzerland)

The neo-Nazi and [white power skinhead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_power_skinhead) scene in Switzerland has seen significant growth in the 1990s and 2000s.[^cite_note-304] It is reflected in the foundation of the [Partei National Orientierter Schweizer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partei_National_Orientierter_Schweizer) in 2000, which resulted in an improved organizational structure of the neo-Nazi and white supremacist scene.

#### Ukraine

See also: [Racism and discrimination in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racism_and_discrimination_in_Ukraine) and [Far-right politics in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far-right_politics_in_Ukraine)

In 1991, the [Social-National Party of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social-National_Party_of_Ukraine) (SNPU) was founded.[^cite_note-305] The party combined [radical nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radical_nationalism) and neo-Nazi features.[^cite_note-konotop-306] The SNPU was characterized as a [radical right-wing populist](8354382.md) party that combined elements of [ethnic ultranationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ultranationalism) and [anti-communism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-communism). During the 1990s, it was accused of neo-Nazism due to the party's recruitment of [skinheads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_power_skinhead) and usage of neo-Nazi symbols.[^cite_note-umlandp41-307][^cite_note-rudling13wr-308] When [Oleh Tyahnybok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleh_Tyahnybok) was elected party leader in 2004, he made efforts to moderate the party's image by changing the party's name to [All-Ukrainian Association "Svoboda"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Svoboda_\(political_party\)), changing its symbols and expelling neo-Nazi and [neofascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-fascism) groups.[^cite_note-osw-tadeusz-309][^cite_note-umlandp41-307] According to radicalism researchers [Anton Shekhovtsov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anton_Shekhovtsov) and [Andreas Umland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andreas_Umland), extreme far-right in Ukraine are extremely weak and marginal force. Right-wing movements researcher [Vyacheslav Likhachev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vyacheslav_Likhachev?action=edit\&redlink=1) notes that the number of Nazi skinheads in 2008 was less than two thousand, which, compared to 20 to 35 thousands skinheads in Russia, makes a substantially lower proportion.[^cite_note-umlandp41-307] According to *[The Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Nation)* journalist James Carden, in 2016 "neo-Nazis (or neo-fascists, if you prefer) are a distinctly minority taste in Western Ukraine".[^cite_note-nation-310] In 2015, Konotop residents elected Artem Semenikhin, a Svoboda party member accused of neo-Nazi sympathies, as a mayor, because, according to Likhachev, he "created himself an image of a defender of Ukrainian independence";[^cite_note-311] however, [Eduard Dolinsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eduard_Isaakovich_Dolinsky?action=edit\&redlink=1) of the [Ukrainian Jewish Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_Jewish_Committee) stated that Konotop was a "clear case" of anti-Semites being elected in local governing bodies.[^cite_note-konotop-306]

The [Azov Battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azov_Battalion), founded in 2014, has been described as a far-right militia,[^cite_note-bbc-20140905-312][^cite_note-telegraph-20220318-313] with connections to neo-Nazism[^cite_note-314] and members wearing neo-Nazi and [SS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schutzstaffel) symbols and regalia, as well as expressing neo-Nazi views.[^cite_note-parfitt-315][^cite_note-Walker-316]

According to Vyacheslav Likhachev of the *[Institut français des relations internationales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institut_fran%C3%A7ais_des_relations_internationales)*, members of far-right (including neo-Nazi) groups played an important role on the pro-Russian side, arguably more so than on the Ukrainian side, especially during early 2014.[^cite_note-Likhachev-317][^cite_note-Averre-318] Members and former members of the [National Bolshevik Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bolshevik_Party), [Russian National Unity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_National_Unity) (RNU), [Eurasian Youth Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurasian_Youth_Union), and [Cossack groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combatants_of_the_war_in_Donbas#Cossacks) participated in recruitment of the separatists.[^cite_note-Likhachev-317][^cite_note-Yudina-319][^cite_note-washingtonp-320][^cite_note-321] A former RNU member, [Pavel Gubarev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pavel_Gubarev), was founder of the Donbas People's Militia and first "governor" of the Donetsk People's Republic.[^cite_note-Likhachev-317][^cite_note-322] RNU is particularly linked to the [Russian Orthodox Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Orthodox_Army),[^cite_note-Likhachev-317] one of a number of separatist units described as "pro-Tsarist" and "extremist" Orthodox nationalists.[^cite_note-Kuzio110-323][^cite_note-Likhachev-317] 'Rusich' is part of the [Wagner Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wagner_Group), a Russian mercenary group in Ukraine which has been linked to far-right extremism.[^cite_note-Guardian_Wagner-324][^cite_note-Smid-325] Afterward, the pro-Russian far-right groups became less important in Donbas and the need for Russian radical nationalists started to disappear.[^cite_note-Likhachev-317]

The radical nationalist group [С14](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C14_\(Ukrainian_group\)), whose members openly expressed neo-Nazi views, gained notoriety in 2018 for being involved in violent attacks on [Romany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romani_people) camps.[^cite_note-326][^cite_note-327][^cite_note-328]

#### United Kingdom

See also: [Far-right politics in the United Kingdom](3884621.md) and [List of British fascist parties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_British_fascist_parties)



[National Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Action_\(UK\)) holding a protest at the [statue of Nelson Mandela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statue_of_Nelson_Mandela,_Parliament_Square)



British [National Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Front_\(UK\)) marchers in the 1970s. It is a far-right, [fascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascism) [political party in the United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_political_parties_in_the_United_Kingdom).

In 1962, the British neo-Nazi activist [Colin Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colin_Jordan) formed the [National Socialist Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialist_Movement_\(UK,_1962\)) (NSM) which later became the [British Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Movement) (BM) in 1968.[^cite_note-Times-329][^cite_note-330]

[John Tyndall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Tyndall_\(politician\)), a long-term neo-Nazi activist in the UK, led a break-away from the [National Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Front_\(UK\)) to form an openly neo-Nazi party named the [British National Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_National_Party).[^cite_note-331] In the 1990s, the party formed a group for protecting its meetings named [Combat 18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combat_18),[^cite_note-Ryan_1998-332] which later grew too violent for the party to control and began to attack members of the BNP who were not perceived as supportive of neo-Nazism.[^cite_note-333] Under the subsequent leadership of [Nick Griffin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Griffin), the BNP distanced itself from neo-Nazism, although many members (including Griffin himself) have been accused of links to other neo-Nazi groups.[^cite_note-334]

[Sonnenkrieg Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonnenkrieg_Division) is a neo-Nazi terrorist organization in the United Kingdom, linked to international [Atomwaffen Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomwaffen_Division) network. Multiple members have been jailed for plotting terror attacks against minorities. Sonnenkrieg Division has been proscribed as a terrorist organization in United Kingdom and Australia. Sonnenkrieg Division is also closely tied with the [Order of Nine Angles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_Nine_Angles) linked to the [Murders of Bibaa Henry and Nicole Smallman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murders_of_Bibaa_Henry_and_Nicole_Smallman).[^cite_note-335][^cite_note-Australia-336][^cite_note-337][^cite_note-338]

The UK has also been a source of neo-Nazi music, such as the band [Skrewdriver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skrewdriver).[^cite_note-339]

### Asia

#### China

There is a neo-Nazi music subculture in China, with government unofficially tolerating pro-[Han](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_Chinese) bands and cracking down on minority artists. Beijing based Goatowarex has many Nazi bands on its label, including Gestapo 666, Satanic Warmaster and Zyklon SS.[^cite_note-340]

According to Global Network on Extremism and Technology, there exists a neo-Nazi movement in China's [Inner Mongolia](55064.md).[^cite_note-341]

#### Indonesia

[Sukarno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukarno) admired [Nazi Germany](21212.md) under Hitler: "It's in the Third Reich that the Germans will see Germany at the apex above other nations in this world," he said in 1963.[^cite_note-342] Al Chaidar, a [terrorism](30636.md) researcher from [Malikussaleh University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malikussaleh_University), estimated that the Neo-Nazi network in Indonesia is around 300 men strong.[^cite_note-343] An Indonesian man influenced by neo-Nazism is suspected of perpetrating the November 2025 [ Jakarta school bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jakarta_school_bombing) in which 97 people were wounded.[^cite_note-inj96-344][^cite_note-345]

#### Iran

See also: [SUMKA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SUMKA), [Aria Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aria_Party), and [Azure Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azure_Party)



Flag of the [SUMKA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SUMKA) (National Socialist Workers Party of Iran)

Several neo-Nazi groups were active in Iran. Advocates of Nazism continue to exist in Iran and are mainly based on the Internet.[^cite_note-346][^cite_note-347]

There were some high-profile Iranian neo-Nazis, for example the [Chief of Staff of the Iranian Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Chiefs_of_Staff_of_the_Iranian_Armed_Forces) [Bahram Aryana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahram_Aryana) held dual membership of [Aria Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aria_Party) and [SUMKA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SUMKA).[^cite_note-348]

In 2026, a neo-Nazi group named Neo Sumka (نئو سومکا in Persian) operates in the country, mixing [Militant accelerationism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Militant_accelerationism), neo-Nazism and Persian racial supremacy.[^cite_note-Koch-349]

#### Iraq



Flag of the Hawpa/PSNK

[Hawpa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawpa), also known as the Kurdish National Socialist Organization (PSNK), is a Kurdish neo-Nazi organization based in [Iraqi Kurdistan](679693.md) that opposes ethnic and sexual minority rights and Arabization and strives for an ethnically pure, united Kurdistan. Hawpa is based on the ideas of [Ramzi Nafi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramzi_Nafi), a Kurdish nationalist and a Nazi collaborator.[^cite_note-350]

#### Israel

Neo-Nazi activity is not common or widespread in Israel, and the few reported activities have all been the work of extremists, who were punished severely. One notable case is that of [Patrol 36](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrol_36), a cell in [Petah Tikva](202411.md) made up of eight teenage immigrants from the former Soviet Union who had been attacking foreign workers and gay people, and vandalizing synagogues with Nazi images.[^cite_note-BBC_Israeli-351][^cite_note-Asser-352] These neo-Nazis were reported to have operated in cities across Israel, and have been described as being influenced by the rise of neo-Nazism in Europe;[^cite_note-BBC_Israeli-351][^cite_note-Asser-352][^cite_note-353] mostly influenced by similar movements in Russia and Ukraine, as the rise of the phenomenon is widely credited to immigrants from those two states, the largest sources of emigration to Israel.[^cite_note-354] Widely publicized arrests have led to a call to reform the [Law of Return](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_Return) to permit the revocation of Israeli citizenship for—and the subsequent deportation of—neo-Nazis.[^cite_note-Asser-352]

#### Japan

Since 1982, the neo-Nazi [National Socialist Japanese Workers' Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialist_Japanese_Workers'_Party) has operated in Japan, currently under the leadership of Kazunari Yamada, who has praised Hitler and denied the Holocaust.[^cite_note-355]

*[Siege](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_\(Mason_book\))*-inspired neo-Nazi group named the New [Co-Prosperity Sphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Co-Prosperity_Sphere) (NCPS) operates in the country and supports [Pan-Asianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-Asianism) and antisemitism and voiced their support for antisemitic attacks perpetrated by their affiliates.[^cite_note-Koch-349]

#### Mongolia



Flag of the Tsagaan Khas, a neo-Nazi party in Mongolia

From 2008, Mongolian neo-Nazi groups have defaced buildings in [Ulaanbaatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulaanbaatar), smashed Chinese shopkeepers' windows, and killed Chinese immigrants. The neo-Nazi Mongols' targets for violence are Chinese, [Koreans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koreans),[^cite_note-naive-356] Mongol women who have sex with Chinese men, and [LGBT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT) people.[^cite_note-mnn-357] They wear Nazi uniforms and revere the [Mongol Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_Empire) and [Genghis Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genghis_Khan). Though [Tsagaan Khas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsagaan_Khas) leaders say they do not support violence, they are self-proclaimed Nazis. "Adolf Hitler was someone we respect. He taught us how to preserve national identity," said the 41-year-old co-founder, who calls himself Big Brother. "We don't agree with his extremism and starting the Second World War. We are against all those killings, but we support his ideology. We support nationalism rather than fascism." Some have ascribed it to poor [historical education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Mongolia).[^cite_note-naive-356]

#### Singapore

In separate incidents in 2020, 2023, 2024 and 2025, Singaporean neo-Nazi accelerationists planned mass killings of muslims, motivated by hatred of Islam and Malay ethnic minority, feeling they threatened the majority Chinese ethnicity. They expressed admiration for [Brenton Tarrant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brenton_Tarrant), some going as far as getting tattoos of Nazi symbols.[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359] Prime Minister [Lawrence Wong](32336575.md) noted in a statement in 2025 that "The range of extremist ideologies has widened. It is no longer limited to religious extremism. We see people drawn to neo-Nazi movements."[^cite_note-360]

[Militant accelerationist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Militant_accelerationist) neo-Nazi group "the New [Co-Prosperity Sphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Co-Prosperity_Sphere) (NCPS)" operates in the country and was responsible for a stabbing attack on Jewish tourists.[^cite_note-Koch-349]

#### Taiwan

Main article: [National Socialism Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialism_Association)

The [National Socialism Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialism_Association) (NSA) is a neo-Nazi political organisation founded in [Taiwan](25734.md) in September 2006 by Hsu Na-chi (Chinese: 許娜琦), at that time a 22-year-old female [political science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_science) graduate of [Soochow University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soochow_University_\(Taiwan\)). The NSA has an explicit stated goal of obtaining the power to govern the state. The [Simon Wiesenthal Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon_Wiesenthal_Centre) condemned the National Socialism Association on 13 March 2007 for championing the former [Nazi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi) dictator and blaming democracy for [social unrest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_unrest) in Taiwan.[^cite_note-Haaretz837967-361]

Even though there is no other significant movement than the officially founded NSA in 2005, the organized neo-Nazi activities have been continuing for years, often causing controversy to the public.[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363] These occasions include a Nazi parades on city streets, in or around the schools,[^cite_note-364][^cite_note-365] restaurants serving dishes honoring Nazis,[^cite_note-BBC-366][^cite_note-367] or displaying *[Mein Kampf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mein_Kampf)* with Nazi dresses while shouting "[Sieg Heil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_salute#Nazi_chants)!" with Nazi salutes.[^cite_note-368] The organized activities were usually condemned by [German Institute Taipei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Institute_Taipei),[^cite_note-369] [Israel Economic and Cultural Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Economic_and_Cultural_Office?action=edit\&redlink=1),[^cite_note-370] and local Jewish communities,[^cite_note-371] and further led to public outcries.[^cite_note-372][^cite_note-373]

#### Turkey

See also: [Turkish nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_nationalism)

A neo-Nazi group existed in 1969 in [İzmir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0zmir), when a group of former [Republican Villagers Nation Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_Villagers_Nation_Party) members (precursor party of the [Nationalist Movement Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalist_Movement_Party)) founded the association "[Nasyonal Aktivite ve Zinde İnkişaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasyonal_Aktivite_ve_Zinde_%C4%B0nki%C5%9Faf)" (*National Activity and Vigorous Development*). The club maintained two combat units. The members wore [SA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sturmabteilung) uniforms and used the [Hitler salute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_salute). One of the leaders (Gündüz Kapancıoğlu) was re-admitted to the Nationalist Movement Party in 1975.[^cite_note-374]

Apart from [neo-fascist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-fascist)[^cite_note-380] [Grey Wolves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grey_Wolves_\(organization\)) and the Turkish [ultranationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ultranationalist)[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383] [Nationalist Movement Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalist_Movement_Party), there are some neo-Nazi organizations in Turkey such as the Turkish Nazi Party[^cite_note-turknazipartisi-384] or the National Socialist Party of Turkey, some of which are mainly based on the Internet.[^cite_note-sabah-385][^cite_note-hurriyet-386][^cite_note-caucasusforum.org-387] As of 2026, several Turkish Telegram groups identifying as “Nasyonal sosyalist" (National Socialist) exist, promoting [Terrorgram](72327036.md) content.[^cite_note-Koch-349]

National Front Party (Ulusal Cephe Partisi) adheres to neo-Nazism, spreads Nazi material translated into Turkish and targets Jews, Arabs and Africans. National Front Party has about 1000 members and is affiliated with the racist [Victory Party (Turkey)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victory_Party_\(Turkey\)).[^cite_note-388]

The neo-Nazi Ataman Brotherhood (Ataman Kardeşliği) patrols streets in Turkey and attacks Syrian and Afghan refugees.[^cite_note-389]

The [Newroz clashes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newroz_clashes) were initiated by a Kurdish Neo-Nazi group known as "Kurdên Nasyonalist", who published a statement before Newroz calling for all Kurdish nationalists to attack Kurdish leftists and the LGBT community and its supporters, who are known to rally at Newroz celebrations.[^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391] Many of the leftists and LGBT supporters were brutally beaten.[^cite_note-392]

The [2024 Eskişehir mosque stabbing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Eski%C5%9Fehir_mosque_stabbing) was perpetrated by a self-described "National Socialist", Arda Küçükyetim.[^cite_note-393]

### Americas

#### Brazil

Main article: [Nazism in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazism_in_Brazil)

Several Brazilian neo-Nazi gangs appeared in the 1990s in [Southern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Region,_Brazil) and [Southeastern Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeast_Region,_Brazil), regions with mostly white people, with their acts gaining more media coverage and public notoriety in the 2010s.[^cite_note-istoe-394][^cite_note-395][^cite_note-396][^cite_note-spgaypride-397] Some members of Brazilian neo-Nazi groups have been associated with [football hooliganism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_hooliganism).[^cite_note-398] Their targets have included African, South American and Asian immigrants; Jews, [Muslims](19541.md), [Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholics) and [atheists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheists); [Afro-Brazilians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afro-Brazilians) and internal migrants with origins in the northern regions of Brazil (who are mostly [brown-skinned](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pardo) or Afro-Brazilian);[^cite_note-nizkor-399][^cite_note-400] [homeless people](19394651.md), [prostitutes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prostitution_in_Brazil); [recreational drug users](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recreational_drug_use); [feminists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feminism) and—more frequently reported in the media—gay people, [bisexuals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bisexuals), and [transgender](19904525.md) and [third-gender](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_gender) people.[^cite_note-spgaypride-397][^cite_note-401][^cite_note-402] News of their attacks has played a role in debates about [anti-discrimination laws in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-discrimination_laws_in_Brazil) (including to some extent [hate speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hate_speech) laws) and the issues of [sexual orientation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_orientation) and [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity).[^cite_note-403][^cite_note-404][^cite_note-405]

#### Canada

Main article: [Neo-Nazism in Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neo-Nazism_in_Canada)

Neo-Nazism in Canada began with the formation of the [Canadian Nazi Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Nazi_Party) in 1965. In the 1970s and 1980s, neo-Nazism continued to spread in the country as organizations including the [Western Guard Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Guard_Party) and [Church of the Creator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creativity_\(religion\)) (later renamed *Creativity*) promoted white supremacist ideals.[^cite_note-source2-406] Founded in the United States in 1973, Creativity calls for [white people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_people) to wage [racial holy war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_holy_war) (Rahowa) against Jews and other perceived enemies.[^cite_note-source14-407]

[Don Andrews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don_Andrews) founded the [Nationalist Party of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalist_Party_of_Canada) in 1977. The purported goals of the unregistered party are "the promotion and maintenance of European Heritage and Culture in Canada," but the party is known for anti-Semitism and racism. Many influential neo-Nazi leaders, such as [Wolfgang Droege](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolfgang_Droege), were affiliated with the party, but many of its members left to join the [Heritage Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heritage_Front), which was founded in 1989.[^cite_note-source3-408]

Droege founded the Heritage Front in [Toronto](64646.md) at a time when leaders of the white supremacist movement were "disgruntled about the state of the radical right" and wanted to unite unorganized groups of white supremacists into an influential and efficient group with common objectives.[^cite_note-source3-408] Plans for the organization began in September 1989, and the formation of the Heritage Front was formally announced a couple of months later in November. In the 1990s, [George Burdi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Burdi) of [Resistance Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resistance_Records) and the band [Rahowa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rahowa_\(band\)) popularized the Creativity movement and the [white power music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_power_music) scene.[^cite_note-409] Heritage Front was part of an alliance of neo-Nazi groups, including the [Aryan Nations](51669.md), Canadian KKK, [Hammerskins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hammerskins) and Creativity movement, among others. Colonel [Gaddafi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaddafi) allegedly fostered the formation of the Front and funded the nationalist alliance. Instead of a hierarchical group, the neo-Nazi network in Canada is highly decentralized. Heritage Front disbanded in 2005.[^cite_note-source3-408]

On September 18, 2020, Toronto Police arrested 34-year-old Guilherme "William" Von Neutegem and charged him with the murder of Mohamed-Aslim Zafis. Zafis was the caretaker of a local mosque who was found dead with his throat cut. The Toronto Police Service said the killing is possibly connected to the stabbing murder of Rampreet Singh a few days prior a short distance from the spot where Zafis' murder took place. Von Neutegem is a member of the [Order of Nine Angles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_Nine_Angles) and social media accounts established as belonging to him promote the group and included recordings of Von Neutegem performing satanic chants. In his home there was also an altar with the symbol of the O9A adorning a monolith.[^cite_note-410] According to Evan Balgord of the Canadian Anti-Hate Network, they are aware of more O9A members in Canada and their affiliated organization Northern Order.[^cite_note-411][^cite_note-412] [Northern Order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Order) is a proscribed[^cite_note-413] neo-Nazi terrorist organization in Canada. NO members have been arrested for trafficking explosives and firearms, and NO has active members of the Canadian Armed Forces as its members and even a member of the [CJIRU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CJIRU) was identified as a member.[^cite_note-414][^cite_note-415][^cite_note-416]

#### Chile

Main article: [Nazism in Chile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazism_in_Chile)



Flag of the [National Socialist Movement of Chile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialist_Movement_of_Chile)

After the dissolution of the [National Socialist Movement of Chile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialist_Movement_of_Chile) (MNSCH) in 1938, notable former members of MNSCH migrated into [Partido Agrario Laborista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partido_Agrario_Laborista) (PAL), obtaining high positions.[^cite_note-etche-417] Not all former MNSCH members joined the PAL; some continued to form parties that followed the MNSCH model until 1952.[^cite_note-etche-417] A new old-school Nazi party was formed in 1964 by school teacher Franz Pfeiffer.[^cite_note-etche-417] Among the activities of this group were the organization of a *Miss Nazi* beauty contest and the formation of a Chilean branch of the [Ku Klux Klan](16779.md).[^cite_note-etche-417] The party disbanded in 1970. Pfeiffer attempted to restart it in 1983 in the wake of a wave of protests against the [Augusto Pinochet regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chile_under_Pinochet).[^cite_note-etche-417]

[Nicolás Palacios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicol%C3%A1s_Palacios) considered the "Chilean race" to be a mix of two bellicose master races: the [Visigoths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visigoth) of Spain and the [Mapuche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mapuche) (Araucanians) of Chile.[^cite_note-palaciosmestizo-418] Palacios traces the origins of the Spanish component of the "Chilean race" to the coast of the [Baltic Sea](3335.md), specifically to [Götaland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%B6taland) in Sweden,[^cite_note-palaciosmestizo-418] one of the supposed [homelands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homeland) of the [Goths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goths). Palacios claimed that both the blonde-haired and the bronze-coloured Chilean [Mestizo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mestizo) share a "moral physonomy" and a masculine psychology.[^cite_note-palaciospsico-419] He opposed immigration from Southern Europe, and argued that Mestizos who are derived from south Europeans lack "cerebral control" and are a social burden.[^cite_note-palacioslatino-420]

#### Costa Rica

Several fringe neo-Nazi groups have existed in [Costa Rica](5551.md), some with online presence since around 2003.[^cite_note-nacion-421][^cite_note-422] The groups normally target Jewish Costa Ricans, [Afro-Costa Ricans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afro-Costa_Rican), [Communists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communism), gay people and especially Nicaraguan and Colombian immigrants. In 2012 the media discovered the existence of a neo-Nazi police officer inside the [Public Force of Costa Rica](5559.md), for which he was fired and would later commit suicide in April 2016 due to lack of job opportunities and threats from [anti-fascists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-fascism).[^cite_note-423][^cite_note-424][^cite_note-425][^cite_note-426]

In 2015, the [Simon Wiesenthal Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon_Wiesenthal_Center) asked the Costa Rican government to shut down a store in [San José](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Jos%C3%A9,_Costa_Rica) that sells Nazi paraphernalia, [Holocaust denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_denial) books and other products associated with [Nazism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazism).[^cite_note-427]
