In 2018, a series of pages on the [social network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_network) Facebook of neo-Nazi inclination openly or discreetly carried out a vast campaign instigating [xenophobic hatred](34141.md) by recycling old news or posting [fake news](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fake_news) to take advantage of an anti-immigrant sentiment after three homicides of tourists allegedly committed by migrants (although from one of the homicides the suspect is Costa Rican).[^cite_note-q-428] A rally against the country's migration policy was held on 19 August 2018, in which neo-Nazi and [hooligans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hooligans) took part. Although not all participants were linked these groups and the majority of participants were peaceful, the protest turned violent and the [Public Force](5559.md) intervened with 44 arrested (36 Costa Ricans and the rest Nicaraguans).[^cite_note-montreal-429][^cite_note-430] Authorities confiscated sharp weapons, [Molotov cocktails](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molotov_cocktails) and other items from the neo-Nazis, who also carried swastika flags.[^cite_note-dw-431] A subsequent anti-xenophobic march and solidarity with the Nicaraguan refugees was organized a week later with more assistance. A second anti-migration demonstration, with the explicit exclusion of neo-Nazis and hooligans, was carried out in September with similar assistance.[^cite_note-432] In 2019 Facebook pages of extreme right-wing tendencies and anti-immigration position as *Deputy 58*, *Costa Rican Resistance* and *Salvation Costa Rica* called an anti-government demonstration on 1 May with small attendance.[^cite_note-433][^cite_note-434]

#### Cuba

Cuban government shut down a rock music cultural center Maxim Rock after a Halloween festival event featuring [SS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SS) uniforms and nazi salutes. In video footage of the event, the attendants applauded a man referred to as the "Fuhrer" who gave a straight arm salute and said "Germany has won".[^cite_note-435]

Some people involved with the [CIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA) sponsored anti-Castro Cuban group [Brigade 2506](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brigade_2506) became involved in neo-Nazism, such as commander [Higinio "Nino" Díaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higinio_%22Nino%22_D%C3%ADaz) who promoted [The International Jew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_International_Jew), said Hitler saved Germany and made the claim that Castro was Jewish.[^cite_note-436] Another member of the Brigade [Felipe Rivero Diaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felipe_Rivero_Diaz) was a known Holocaust denier, antisemite and an associate of [David Duke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Duke).[^cite_note-437][^cite_note-\:5-438]

#### Mexico

Neo-Nazi groups have grown in [Mexico](3966054.md) since the beginning of the 21st century on internet forums. Especially in the case of skinheads, they are often divided into two groups: the [Caucasian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Mexicans) population of European descent and the [mestizos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mestizos_in_Mexico), the latter pejoratively referred to as "[morenazis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morenazi)."[^cite_note-439]

In the 2010s and 2020s, there have been cases of pro-Nazi rock bands holding clandestine concerts in major Mexican cities, many of which openly displayed associated symbols such as the [swastika](45943.md) or [Adolf Hitler](2731583.md)'s cult of personality.[^cite_note-440][^cite_note-441] According to *Metal-Archives*, at least 28 neo-Nazi black metal bands have been identified in the country, 21 of which are active.[^cite_note-442]

#### Peru

Peru has been home to a handful of neo-Nazi groups, most notably the National Socialist Movement "Peru Awake", the National Socialist Tercios of New Castile, and the Peruvian National Socialist Union.[^cite_note-443][^cite_note-444][^cite_note-445]

#### United States

Further information: [Fascism in the United States § Neo-Nazism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascism_in_the_United_States#Neo-Nazism), and [Radical right (United States)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radical_right_\(United_States\))



[National Socialist Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialist_Movement_\(United_States\)) rally on the west lawn of the [US Capitol](31979.md), Washington, DC, 2008



[Blood Tribe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood_Tribe_\(neo-Nazi_group\)) demonstrating in Florida, 2025

##### Societal acceptance

In 2017, following the [Charlottesville car attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlottesville_car_attack), an [ABC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABC_News_\(United_States\))/[Washington Post](102226.md) [poll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opinion_poll) found that 9% of Americans considered having neo-Nazi beliefs was acceptable; at the time, this amounted to 22 million Americans.[^cite_note-446]

###### Elite and political legitimization

On January 25, 2026 the [Lemkin Institute for Genocide Prevention and Human Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lemkin_Institute_for_Genocide_Prevention) published a "Statement on the Normalization of Nazism in the U.S Government" in which the Institute expressed alarm over legitimization and normalization of Nazi-associated rhetoric, symbolism, and white supremacism by the administration. Neo-Nazi groups like the [Aryan Freedom Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aryan_Freedom_Network) and [The Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Base_\(neo-Nazi_group\)) are also reportedly expanding recruitment and activities amid reduced federal scrutiny.[^cite_note-447]

##### Ideology

The ideology of [James H. Madole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_H._Madole), leader of the [National Renaissance Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Renaissance_Party_\(United_States\)), was influenced by [Blavatskian Theosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theosophy_\(Blavatskian\)). [Helena Blavatsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helena_Blavatsky) developed a racial theory of [evolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evolution), holding that the [white race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_race) was the "fifth rootrace" called the [Aryan race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aryan_race). According to Blavatsky, Aryans had been preceded by Atlanteans who had perished in the flood that sunk the continent [Atlantis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantis). The three races that preceded the Atlanteans, in Blavatsky's view, were proto-humans; these were the [Lemurians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lemuria_\(continent\)), [Hyperboreans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperborea) and the first Astral rootrace. It was on this foundation that Madole based his claims that the Aryan race has been worshiped as "White Gods" since time immemorial and proposed a governance structure based on the Hindu [Laws of Manu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laws_of_Manu) and its hierarchical [caste system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caste_system_in_India).[^cite_note-448]

##### Organizations and individuals

There are several neo-Nazi groups in the United States. The [National Socialist Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialist_Movement_\(United_States\)) (NSM)[^cite_note-449] was one of largest neo-Nazi organization in the US. NSM had 400 members at its peak but is now a fraction of it.[^cite_note-backgroundernsm-450] After World War II, new organizations formed with varying degrees of support for Nazi principles. The [National States' Rights Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_States'_Rights_Party), founded in 1958 by Edward Reed Fields and [J. B. Stoner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._B._Stoner), countered [racial integration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_integration) in the Southern United States with Nazi-inspired publications and iconography. The [American Nazi Party](364176.md), founded by [George Lincoln Rockwell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Lincoln_Rockwell) in 1959, achieved high-profile coverage in the press through its public demonstrations.[^cite_note-451]

The [Institute for Historical Review](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_Historical_Review), formed in 1978, is a [Holocaust denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_denial) body associated with neo-Nazism.[^cite_note-ADL-452]

Groups like the terrorist group [Atomwaffen Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomwaffen_Division) grew after the [Unite the Right rally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unite_the_Right_rally), recruiting those radicalized by its failure.[^cite_note-453][^cite_note-454] Atomwaffen Division has been linked to at least 11 murders.[^cite_note-455]

In 2022, famous rapper [Kanye West](523032.md) stated that he identifies as a Nazi, denying the Holocaust and praising the policies of Adolf Hitler.[^cite_note-456]

In 2025, [Elon Musk](909036.md) was widely criticized by governments, media outlets, and watchdog groups after making [a gesture during a public speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elon_Musk_salute_controversy) that many interpreted as a Nazi salute, intensifying concerns about his association with extremist views and antisemitic conspiracy theories.[^cite_note-Condon_2025-457]

According to the Terrorism Research and Analysis Consortium, the Aryan Freedom Network has as many as 1,500 members as of late 2025, which would make it the biggest neo-Nazi group in the US.[^cite_note-458]

##### Between freedom of speech and national security threats

The [First Amendment to the United States Constitution](31653.md) guarantees [freedom of speech](21401843.md), which the courts have interpreted very broadly to include [hate speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hate_speech), severely limiting the government's authority to suppress it.[^cite_note-459] This allows political organizations great latitude in expressing Nazi, racist, and antisemitic views. A landmark First Amendment case was *[National Socialist Party of America v. Village of Skokie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialist_Party_of_America_v._Village_of_Skokie)*, in which neo-Nazis threatened to march in a predominantly Jewish suburb of Chicago. The march never took place in Skokie, but the court ruling allowed the neo-Nazis to stage a series of demonstrations in Chicago.

Organizations which report upon neo-Nazi activities in the U.S., which may involve attacking and harassing minorities, include the American organizations [Anti-Defamation League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Defamation_League) and the [Southern Poverty Law Center](27164.md).[^cite_note-460]

In 2020, the [FBI](11127.md) reclassified neo-Nazis to the same threat level as ISIS. [Chris Wray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_A._Wray), the [Director of the Federal Bureau of Investigation](5861065.md), stated "Not only is the terror threat diverse, it's unrelenting."[^cite_note-461][^cite_note-462]

#### Uruguay

In 1998, a group of people belonging to the "Joseph Goebbels Movement" tried to burn down a synagogue, which also served as a Hebrew school, in the Pocitos neighborhood of [Montevideo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montevideo) in Uruguay; an [antisemitic](1078.md) pamphlet signed by the group was found in the building after the quick action of firefighters saved it. Another group, the racist and antisemitic neo-Nazi *Euroamerikaners* group, founded in 1996, said when they were interviewed by the newspaper *La República de Montevideo* that they had no involvement with the attack on the synagogue, but revealed that they maintain contacts with a group called *Poder Blanco* ("White Power"), also Uruguayan, as well as with neo-Nazi groups from [Argentina](18951905.md) and several European countries. Through the Internet they have received the solidarity of the *Patria* pro-fascist group, based in Spain. They also said that in the city of [Canelones, Uruguay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canelones,_Uruguay), fifty kilometers from Montevideo, there is a clandestine "[Aryan church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aryan_Christianity)" which uses rituals taken from the [Ku Klux Klan](16779.md). The *Euroamerikaners* declared that they did not tolerate interracial or gay couples. One of the militants said in the interview that "... if we see a black man with a white woman, we break them up ...". Other neo-Nazi incidents in Uruguay in 1998 included the bombing of a Jewish-owned small business in February, which injured two people, and the appearance of posters celebrating the anniversary of Hitler's birthday in April.[^cite_note-clarin-463]

### Africa

#### South Africa

Several groups in South Africa, such as [Afrikaner Weerstandsbeweging](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afrikaner_Weerstandsbeweging) and [Blanke Bevrydingsbeweging](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blanke_Bevrydingsbeweging), have often been described as neo-Nazi.[^cite_note-afrikaner-464] [Eugène Terre'Blanche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eug%C3%A8ne_Terre'Blanche) was a prominent South African neo-Nazi leader who was murdered in 2010.[^cite_note-465]

### Oceania

#### Australia



Members of the [National Socialist Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialist_Network) making [Nazi salutes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_salutes) on 18 March 2023

There were a number of now-defunct [Australian](4689264.md) neo-Nazi groups, such as the [Australian National Socialist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_National_Socialist_Party) (ANSP), which was formed in 1962 and merged into the [National Socialist Party of Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialist_Party_of_Australia) (1968–1970s), originally a splinter group, in 1968,[^cite_note-Henderson_76-466] and [Jack van Tongeren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_van_Tongeren)'s Australian Nationalist Movement.[^cite_note-Henderson_76-466]

The [National Socialist Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialist_Network) (NSN) is an Australian neo-Nazi political organisation formed from two far-right organisations, the [Lads Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lads_Society) and the [Antipodean Resistance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antipodean_Resistance), in 2020.

White supremacist organisations active in Australia as of 2016 included local chapters of the Aryan Nations.[^cite_note-467] [Blair Cottrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blair_Cottrell), former leader of the [United Patriots Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Patriots_Front), has tried to distance himself from neo-Nazism, but he has nevertheless been accused of expressing "pro-Nazi views".[^cite_note-468] [Australian Security Intelligence Organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Security_Intelligence_Organisation) director [Mike Burgess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Burgess_\(intelligence_chief\)) said in February 2020 that neo-Nazis pose a "real threat" to Australia's security. Burgess maintained that there is a growing threat from the extreme right, and that its supporters "regularly meet to salute Nazi flags, inspect weapons, train in combat and share their hateful ideology".[^cite_note-469] In June 2022, the Australian state [Victoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_\(Australia\)) banned display of the swastika symbol. Under the new law, individuals who intentionally exhibit the symbol may face up to a year in jail or a A\$22,000 (£12,300; \$15,000) fine. The state of Victoria already has laws against hate speech, but they have been criticized for having weaknesses. The call for reform of these laws grew stronger in 2020 when a couple flew a swastika flag over their home, causing outrage in the community.[^cite_note-470]

#### New Zealand

In [New Zealand](4913064.md), historical neo-Nazi organisations include [Unit 88](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unit_88)[^cite_note-471] and the [National Socialist Party of New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Socialist_Party_of_New_Zealand).[^cite_note-472] White nationalist organisations such as the [New Zealand National Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_National_Front) and [Action Zealandia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Action_Zealandia) have faced accusations of neo-Nazism.[^cite_note-473]

The main organizers and guests of the event have been drawn from either non-party-affiliated far-right-activists or members of the right-wing populist Finns Party (Perussuomalaiset), its youth organization Finns Party Youth (Perussuomalaiset Nuoret)...the 612-march is a torchlight procession from central Helsinki to the Hietaniemi war cemetery, where members visit the tomb of World War II-era President Carl Gustaf Emil Mannerheim and the monument to the Finnish SS-Battalion.
